will only be non-zero for very particular representations
\rho
and specific choices of
\beta
. In those cases however, tautological systems are particularly interesting.
The aim of this section is therefore to develop general criteria for vanishing resp. non-vanishing of tautological systems.

4.1
\mathscr{D}
-modules from group actions

Here we consider the action of a linear algebraic group
G^{\prime}
on a variety
Y
.
The main case of interest (which will be discussed from
Section

4.4
on)
arises when we are given an action of a reductive linear algebraic group
G
on a smooth variety
X
, and an equivariant line bundle
\mathscr{L}
on
X
. Denoting by
G^{\prime}
the group
G\times\mathds{C}^{*}
, we let
G^{\prime}
act on
Y
, which we take to be the total space
L
(or the complement
L^{*}
of the zero section) of
\mathscr{L}
. For the purpose of clarity, it is however useful to first treat the case of an arbitrary variety
Y
admitting a
G^{\prime}
-action, where
G^{\prime}
is any linear algebraic group. This is the point of view that we are going to adapt in Sections
4.1
to
4.3
.

We begin by recalling some facts concerning group actions on smooth varieties. They are mainly included for the reader’s convenience and in order to fix notations. The proofs are rather elementary and will therefore be omitted.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
G^{\prime}
be an algebraic group acting on a smooth variety
Y
. Then there is a unique Lie algebra homomorphism

Z_{Y}\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Gamma(Y,\Theta_{Y})

associating to every element
\xi
of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
of
G^{\prime}
a vector field
Z_{Y}(\xi)
on
Y
with the following point-wise description: At a point
y\in Y
, the tangent vector of the vector field
Z_{Y}(\xi)
is given by
\differential\varphi^{y}(\xi)
, where
\varphi^{y}\colon G^{\prime}\to Y
,
g\mapsto g^{-1}\cdot y
, and
\xi
is understood as a tangent vector to
G^{\prime}
at the point
1\in G^{\prime}
.

In the complex analytic category, the vector field
Z_{Y}(\xi)
may be defined as the derivation

Z_{Y}(\xi)(f)=\frac{d}{dt}{f\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exp(t\xi)^{-1}\cdot{(-)}}}\right)_{|{t=0}}}.

If the
G^{\prime}
-variety
Y
considered is clear from the context, we will drop the index and just write
Z(\xi)
. In the literature, the vector field
Z(\xi)
is sometimes denoted by
L_{\xi}
, see e.g.
[
Hot98
, II.2]
.

Example 4.2
.

Consider the action of
G^{\prime}
on itself by left-multiplication (i.e.,
Y=G^{\prime}
). Then
-Z_{G^{\prime}}(\xi)
is the right-invariant vector field associated to
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. If, for example,
G^{\prime}=(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}
and
\xi\in\mathds{C}^{d}=\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
, then

Z_{(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}}(\xi)=-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}\,t_{i}\partial_{t_{i}},

where
(t_{1},\dots,t_{d})
are the standard coordinates on
(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}
.

For group actions on finite-dimensional vector spaces, we also have the following description:

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\rho\colon G^{\prime}\to\GL(V)
be a finite-dimensional rational representation of an algebraic group
G^{\prime}
. The induced left action of
G^{\prime}
on
\mathds{C}[V]=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\Sym^{d}V^{\vee}
describes a morphism of algebraic groups
G^{\prime}\to\GL_{\mathds{C}}(\mathds{C}[V])
whose induced Lie algebra homomorphism
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\End_{\mathds{C}}(\mathds{C}[V])
makes the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathds{C}}(\mathds{C}[V])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Der(\mathds{C}[V])}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{V}}

Explicitly, if we fix coordinates
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
on
V
and consider the associated Lie algebra representation
\differential\rho\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{gl}(V)=\mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathds{C})=\mathds{C}^{n\times n}
, then

Z_{V}(\xi)=-\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\differential\rho(\xi)_{ji}\,x_{i}\partial_{x_{j}}

for all
\xi\in\Lie(G^{\prime})
.

Example 4.4
.

Let
G^{\prime}=(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}
be a
d
-dimensional torus acting linearly on an
n
-dimensional vector space
V
. We identify
V
with
\mathds{C}^{n}
by picking a basis that diagonalizes the action, i.e.,
t=(t_{1},\dots,t_{d})\in(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}
acts on
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in\mathds{C}^{n}
by

t\cdot x=(t^{\alpha_{1}}x_{1},\dots,t^{\alpha_{n}}x_{n})\qquad\text{with }\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}.

If
\xi\in\mathds{Z}^{d}=\Lie\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}}}\right)
is the
i
-th standard basis vector
e_{i}
, we get the vector field

Z_{V}(e_{i})=-\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\alpha_{j})_{i}x_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}

on
V
. These are the vector fields showing up in GKZ-systems associated to the given torus action.

Example 4.5
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathds{P}^{k}
be the rational normal curve of degree
k
, i.e., the image of

\mathds{P}^{1}\xrightarrow{|\mathcal{O}(k)|}\mathds{P}^{k},\qquad[x_{0}:x_{1}]\mapsto\big[{\textstyle\binom{k}{i}\,x_{0}^{k-i}x_{1}^{i}\mid i=0,\dots,k}\big],

and let
Y:=\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}
be the punctured affine cone over
X
in
V:=\mathds{C}^{k+1}
. The group
\SL(2)
acts on
V=H^{0}(\mathds{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}(k))^{\vee}=\Sym^{k}(\mathds{C}^{2})
, the
k
-th symmetric power of the standard
\SL(2)
-representation, and we extend this to an action of
G^{\prime}:=\SL(2)\times\mathds{C}^{*}
by letting the
\mathds{C}^{*}
-factor act by scaling on
V
.
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
is generated by
E_{12},E_{21},E_{11}-E_{22}\in\mathfrak{sl}(2)
and the generator
\mathbf{e}
of
\Lie(\mathds{C}^{*})\cong\mathds{C}
.
The induced vector fields on
V
are

\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{3em}}c}\begin{aligned} Z_{V}(E_{12})&=-\sum_{i=1}^{k}i\,z_{i}\partial_{z_{i-1}},\\
Z_{V}(E_{11}-E_{22})&=-\sum_{i=0}^{k}(k-2i)\,z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}},\end{aligned}\hfil\hskip 30.00005pt&\begin{aligned} Z_{V}(E_{21})&=-\sum_{i=1}^{k}(k-i+1)\,z_{i-1}\partial_{z_{i}},\\
Z_{V}(\mathbf{e})&=-\sum_{i=0}^{k}z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}},\end{aligned}\end{array}

where
z_{0},\dots,z_{k}
denote the coordinates on
V=\mathds{C}^{k+1}
. Note that the minus signs appear because we differentiate the
contragredient
action on the coordinate ring of
V
.

On the
G^{\prime}
-invariant subset
Y
, these vector fields restrict to the vector fields
Z_{Y}(\xi)
. In local charts, these can be expressed as follows: We may cover
Y
by the two open subsets
U_{0}
and
U_{1}
given by the non-vanishing of
x_{0}^{k}\in V^{\vee}
and
x_{1}^{k}\in V^{\vee}
, respectively. Identifying

\displaystyle U_{0}

\displaystyle\cong\mathds{C}^{*}\times\mathds{C},\qquad\lambda\cdot{\textstyle(1,ks,\binom{k}{2}s^{2},\dots,ks^{k-1},s^{k})}\quad\mapsfrom\ (\lambda,s),

\displaystyle U_{1}

\displaystyle\cong\mathds{C}^{*}\times\mathds{C},\qquad\mu\cdot{\textstyle(t^{k},kt^{k-1},\binom{k}{2}t^{k-2},\dots,kt,1)}\ \,\mapsfrom\ (\mu,t),

the vector fields induced from the
G^{\prime}
-action on
Y
are:

\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{3em}}c}\begin{aligned} {Z_{Y}(E_{12})_{|{U_{0}}}}&=-ks\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+s^{2}\partial_{s},\\
{Z_{Y}(E_{21})_{|{U_{0}}}}&=-\partial_{s},\\
{Z_{Y}(E_{11}-E_{22})_{|{U_{0}}}}&=-k\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+2s\partial_{s},\\
{Z_{Y}(\mathbf{e})_{|{U_{0}}}}&=-\lambda\partial_{\lambda},\end{aligned}\hfil\hskip 30.00005pt&\begin{aligned} {Z_{Y}(E_{12})_{|{U_{1}}}}&=-\partial_{t},\\
{Z_{Y}(E_{21})_{|{U_{1}}}}&=-kt\mu\partial_{\mu}+t^{2}\partial_{t},\\
{Z_{Y}(E_{11}-E_{22})_{|{U_{1}}}}&=k\mu\partial_{\mu}-2t\partial_{t},\\
{Z_{Y}(\mathbf{e})_{|{U_{1}}}}&=-\mu\partial_{\mu}.\end{aligned}\end{array}

Note that these local expressions coincide on the intersection
U_{0}\cap U_{1}
under the gluing

\mathds{C}^{*}\times\mathds{C}^{*}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathds{C}^{*}\times\mathds{C}^{*}
,
(\lambda,s)\mapsto(\lambda s^{k},s^{-1})=(\mu,t)
.

Using the vector fields defined
4.1
, we introduce the following
\mathscr{D}
-modules:

Definition 4.6
.

Let
G^{\prime}
be an algebraic group acting on a smooth variety
Y
and let
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
be a Lie algebra homomorphism. Then we define the left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module

\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}:=\omega_{Y}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}/(Z_{Y}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{Y}.

Remark 4.7
.

In case that the action of
G^{\prime}
on
Y
is transitive, it is easy to see that if
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}\neq 0
, then it is a smooth
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module of rank
1
(namely, the vector fields
Z_{Y}(\xi)
, when
\xi
runs through
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
, generate the tangent bundle of
Y
). As already mentioned, our main case of interest is when
Y=L^{*}
for some
{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times G
-equivariant line bundle
L
on a homogeneous
G
-space
X
. Then
G^{\prime}:=\mathds{C}^{*}\times G
clearly acts transitively on
L^{*}
, and therefore
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
corresponds to a rank
1
local system on
L^{*}
. By the discussion in
Section

2
, we then know that
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
must be one of the
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-modules introduced in
2.2
. Under the hypothesis that
G
is semisimple, one can also show that then
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}
. However, one of the main points in this section is that very often, the modules
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
(and, if
Y
is an orbit in a representation space
V
of
G^{\prime}
, the tautological system
\tau(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
) is zero, and then it is certainly not isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}
. We will develop below criteria that guarantee the non-vanishing of the modules
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
resp. of tautological systems (see
4.33
and
4.34
below).

Example 4.8
.

Let
G^{\prime}=T=(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}
be a
d
-dimensional torus acting on itself. We identify Lie algebra homomorphisms
\beta\colon\mathds{C}^{d}=\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
with vectors
\beta\in\mathds{C}^{d}
. Then

\mathscr{N}_{T}^{\beta}=\omega_{T}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}\mathscr{D}_{T}/(-t_{i}\partial_{t_{i}}-\beta_{i}\mid i=1,\dots,d)\mathscr{D}_{T}\cong\mathscr{D}_{T}/\mathscr{D}_{T}(\partial_{t_{i}}t_{i}-\beta_{i}\mid i=1,\dots,d).

This
\mathscr{D}_{T}
-module was called
\mathcal{O}_{T}^{-\beta}
in
[
RS20
]
.

Example 4.9
.

We reconsider the action of
G^{\prime}=\SL(2)\times\mathds{C}^{*}
on the punctured affine cone
Y
over the rational normal curve of degree
k
from
example

4.5
and use the notations from before. Every Lie algebra homomorphism
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
is given by
{\beta_{|{\mathfrak{sl}(2)}}}\equiv 0
and
\beta(\mathbf{e})=\beta_{0}\in\mathds{C}
. By the computations in
example

4.5
, in the local chart
U_{0}\cong\mathds{C}^{*}\times\mathds{C}\subseteq Y
, the
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{(\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta})_{|{U_{0}}}}

\displaystyle\cong\omega_{U_{0}}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{0}}}\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}/(-ks\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+s^{2}\partial_{s},\;-\partial_{s},\;-k\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+2s\partial_{s},\;-\lambda\partial_{\lambda}-\beta_{0})\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}

\displaystyle\cong\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}/\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}(ks\partial_{\lambda}\lambda-\partial_{s}s^{2},\;\partial_{s},\;k\partial_{\lambda}\lambda-2\partial_{s}s,\;\partial_{\lambda}\lambda-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle\cong\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}/\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}(ks\lambda\partial_{\lambda}-s^{2}\partial_{s}+(k-2)s,\;\partial_{s},\;k\lambda\partial_{\lambda}-2s\partial_{s}+(k-2),\;\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+1-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle\cong\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}/\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}(\partial_{s},\;\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+1-\beta_{0},\;k(-1+\beta_{0})+(k-2))

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\mathscr{D}_{\mathds{C}^{*}}/\mathscr{D}_{\mathds{C}^{*}}(\partial_{\lambda}\lambda-\beta_{0})\boxtimes\mathscr{D}_{\mathds{C}}/\mathscr{D}_{\mathds{C}}\cdot\partial_{s}\quad&\text{if }\beta_{0}=2/k,\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and similarly for the other local chart
U_{1}
of
Y
. In particular, for one specific value for
\beta(\mathbf{e})
, we obtain a non-zero
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module that will be of interest to us.

Note that in contrast, if we define the cyclic
left
module

\tilde{\mathscr{N}}_{Y}^{\beta}:=\mathscr{D}_{Y}/\mathscr{D}_{Y}(Z_{Y}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}),

then, in this example, we get

\displaystyle{(\tilde{\mathscr{N}}_{Y}^{\beta})_{|{U_{0}}}}

\displaystyle\cong\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}/\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}(-ks\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+s^{2}\partial_{s},\;-\partial_{s},\;-k\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+2s\partial_{s},\;-\lambda\partial_{\lambda}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle\cong\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}/\mathscr{D}_{U_{0}}(\partial_{s},\;\lambda\partial_{\lambda}+\beta_{0},\;k\beta_{0})\cong\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0}}&\text{if }\beta_{0}=0,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

For
k=2
, we have
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{(\beta_{0}=1)}=\tilde{\mathscr{N}}_{Y}^{(\beta_{0}=0)}
, but in general they do not agree with each other. In fact, one can show that although
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
is
locally
a cyclic left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module, it does not admit a
global
description as a cyclic left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module for
k\geq 3
.

The main reason we wish to consider the
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
defined via the right-left-transformation of a cyclic right-module is the following behavior under equivariant closed embeddings:

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
G^{\prime}
be an algebraic group and let
i\colon Y_{1}\hookrightarrow Y_{2}
be a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant closed embedding between smooth
G^{\prime}
-varieties
Y_{1},Y_{2}
.
Then, for all Lie algebra homomorphisms
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
, we have

i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y_{1}}^{\beta}\cong\omega_{Y_{2}}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}/\big(\mathcal{I}+(Z_{Y_{2}}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\big)\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}

where
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}
is the ideal sheaf of
Y_{1}
in
Y_{2}
.

Proof.

Since
i\colon Y_{1}\hookrightarrow Y_{2}
is a closed embedding, the functor
i_{*}
is exact and the transfer module
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}
is a flat
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}
-module. Therefore, the direct image of
\mathscr{N}_{Y_{1}}^{\beta}
under
i
is given by

i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y_{1}}^{\beta}\cong\omega_{Y_{2}}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}}i_{*}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}/(Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}\big).

Hence, the claim is that

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}/(Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\cong}}i^{-1}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}/((Z_{Y_{2}}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}+\mathcal{I}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}})\big).

as right
i^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}
-modules.
Note that
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}\cong i^{-1}(\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}/\mathcal{I}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}})
as right
i^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}
-modules, since
i
is a closed embedding. Under the left
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}
-module structure on
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}
, vector fields on
Y_{1}
act via the push-forward homomorphism

\differential i\colon\Theta_{Y_{1}}\to i^{*}\Theta_{Y_{2}}=\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}\otimes_{i^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}}i^{-1}\Theta_{Y_{2}}\cong i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}/\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}}\Theta_{Y_{2}}).

We note that the push-forward of the vector field
Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi)
on
Y_{1}
agrees with the restriction of the vector field
Z_{Y_{2}}(\xi)
on
Y_{2}
to
Y_{1}
, i.e.,
\differential i(Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi))=1\otimes Z_{Y_{2}}(\xi)
. Indeed, this follows from the construction of
Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi)
and
Z_{Y_{2}}(\xi)
, using the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}\times Y_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}\times Y_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{G^{\prime}}\times i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}

where
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}
are the morphisms given by the
G^{\prime}
-actions.

This shows that
Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi)\in\Der(\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}})
acts on the right
i^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}
-module
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}\cong i^{-1}(\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}/\mathcal{I}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}})
by left-multiplication with
Z_{Y_{2}}(\xi)
. This implies the claimed description as a cyclic right
i^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{2}}
-module of
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}/(Z_{Y_{1}}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi)\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}}}\mathscr{D}_{Y_{1}\to Y_{2}}
, concluding the proof.
∎

The
\mathscr{D}
-modules in
4.10
look similar to the
\beta
-twistedly equivariant
\mathscr{D}
-modules considered in
[
Hot98
, II.2]
, yet they are different: Instead of considering a cyclic
left
module obtained by quotienting out a
G^{\prime}
-stable ideal and the vector fields induced by the group action (twisted with
\beta
), we instead consider the
right
module constructed in the same way and apply a right-left transformation to obtain a left
\mathscr{D}
-module. The behavior under direct images of closed embeddings in
4.10
is the reason why for our purposes we work with the definition via right modules in
4.6
.

We next consider the situation where
Y
is an orbit
of a rational representation
\rho
of our group
G^{\prime}
in a given vector space
V
. Recall from our basic
1.1
that under this hypothesis, we can define, for any Lie algebra homomorphism
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathds{C}}
, the
\mathscr{D}_{V}
-module
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
(as well as its Fourier-Laplace transform
\tau(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
which was called tautological system in
1.1
). The next result tells us about a technically easy but imporant relation of this
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
to the
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
considered above.

Corollary 4.11
.

Let
\rho\colon G^{\prime}\to\GL(V)
be a finite-dimensional rational representation of an algebraic group and let
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
be a Lie algebra homomorphism. Let
Y\subseteq V
be a
G^{\prime}
-orbit, let
\overline{Y}
be its closure and let
\partial Y:=\overline{Y}\setminus Y
.
Then

j^{+}\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)\cong i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta},

where
Y=\overline{Y}\setminus\partial Y\xhookrightarrow{i}U:=V\setminus\partial Y\xhookrightarrow{j}V
.

In particular, if
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
is localized at
\partial Y
(meaning
j_{+}j^{+}\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)\cong\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
), then it is the direct image of
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
under the locally closed embedding
Y\hookrightarrow V
.

Proof.

We apply
4.10
to the
G^{\prime}
-spaces
Y_{1}:=Y
,
Y_{2}:=V\setminus\partial Y
and the closed embedding
i\colon Y_{1}\hookrightarrow Y_{2}
to see that

i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}\cong\omega_{U}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\mathscr{D}_{U}/\big(\mathcal{I}+\{Z_{U}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\}\big)\mathscr{D}_{U}.

Choosing coordinates
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
on
V
, we may by
4.3
express the vector field
Z_{U}(\xi)
as the derivation
-\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\differential\rho(\xi)_{ji}\,x_{i}\partial_{x_{j}}
. The right-left transformation
\omega_{U}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}(\cdot)
is then explicitly given by transposing operators:

i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}\cong\mathscr{D}_{U}/\mathscr{D}_{U}\big(\mathcal{I}+\{Z_{U}(\xi)^{T}-\beta(\xi)\}\big).

An explicit computation of the transposed vector fields yields:

Z_{V}(\xi)^{T}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\differential\rho(\xi)_{ji}\,\partial_{x_{j}}x_{i}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\differential\rho(\xi)_{ji}\,x_{i}\partial_{x_{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\differential\rho(\xi)_{ii}=-Z_{V}(\xi)+\trace(\differential\rho(\xi)),

hence (using
Z_{V}(\xi)_{|U}=Z_{U}(\xi)
) we have that
i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}\cong j^{+}\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
.
∎

Example 4.12

(GKZ-systems)
.

Consider a torus representation
\rho\colon(\mathds{C}^{*})^{n}\to\GL(n,\mathds{C})
that is given by
\rho(t_{1},\dots,t_{d})=\diag(t^{\alpha_{1}},\dots,t^{\alpha_{n}})
with
\alpha_{i}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}
. Let
\overline{Y}\subseteq\mathds{C}^{n}
be the orbit closure of the point
(1,\dots,1)\in\mathds{C}^{n}
; this is a (not necessarily normal) affine toric variety. The
\mathscr{D}_{\mathds{C}^{n}}
-module
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
is the Fourier–Laplace transform
\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{A}(-\beta)
of the GKZ-system
\mathscr{M}_{A}(-\beta)
(see, e.g.
[
RSSW21
]
for an overview and for the notation used here), where
A
is the
d\times n
-matrix whose
i
-th column is
\alpha_{i}
and
\beta\colon\Lie\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}}}\right)=\mathds{Z}^{d}\to\mathds{C}
is identified with the vector
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\beta(e_{i})}}\right)_{i=1,\dots,d}\in\mathds{C}^{d}
.

In this case,
4.11
applied to
Y=\overline{Y}\cap(\mathds{C}^{*})^{n}
says that
\widehat{\mathscr{M}}_{A}(-\beta)
is the direct image of
\mathscr{N}_{(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}}^{\beta}=\mathcal{O}_{(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}}^{-\beta}
under the locally closed embedding
(\mathds{C}^{*})^{d}\cong Y\hookrightarrow\mathds{C}^{n}
, whenever
\mathscr{M}_{A}(-\beta)
is localized at the intersection of
Y
with the union of coordinate hyperplanes of
\mathds{C}^{n}
. This was observed in
[
SW09
]
, where an explicit combinatorial characterization of the localization property in terms of
A
and
\beta
was proved using Euler–Koszul complexes.

Example 4.13
.

Reconsider from
example

4.5
the punctured affine cone
Y
over the rational normal curve of degree
k
. This may be identified with the complement of the zero section in the line bundle
L=\Tot(\mathcal{O}_{\mathds{P}^{1}}(k))\to\mathds{P}^{1}
. The calculation in
example

4.9
shows that
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}=\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{-\beta(\mathbf{e})}
if
\beta(\mathbf{e})=2/k
and
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}=0
otherwise.
4.11
shows that the restriction of the FL-transformed tautological system

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)=\mathscr{D}_{V}/\mathscr{D}_{V}\cdot

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\binom{k}{i_{2}}\binom{k}{j_{2}}z_{i_{1}}z_{j_{1}}-\binom{k}{i_{1}}\binom{k}{j_{1}}z_{i_{2}}z_{j_{2}}\mid i_{1}+j_{1}=i_{2}+j_{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\cup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{-\sum_{i=1}^{k}i\,z_{i}\partial_{z_{i-1}},\;-\sum_{i=1}^{k}(k-i+1)\,z_{i-1}\partial_{z_{i}},\;}}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\cup{}\bigg\{}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left.-\sum_{i=0}^{k}(k-2i)\,z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}},\;-\sum_{i=0}^{k}z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}}-(k+1)+\beta(\mathbf{e})}}\right\}

to the complement of the origin in
V
is

{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)_{|{V\setminus\{0\}}}}=\begin{cases}i_{+}\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{-\beta(\mathbf{e})}&\text{if }\beta(\mathbf{e})=2/k,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

4.2
\mathscr{D}
-modules from equivariant line bundles

The non-vanishing of tautological systems is by
4.11
closely tied to the non-vanishing of the
\mathscr{D}
-modules
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
. The aim of this section is to study criteria for
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
to be (non-)zero. In order to do so, we introduce another construction of
\mathscr{D}
-modules on a
G^{\prime}
-variety
Y
, which also depends on the choice of an equivariant line bundle on
Y
. It will turn out (this is the main result of
Section

4.3
below) that the modules
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
can be expressed in exactly this way.
For such
\mathscr{D}
-modules defined by equivariant line bundles, it is possible to develop non-vanishing criteria using an interpretation via modules over rings of twisted differential operators (called
\mathscr{A}
-modules below).

We continue with the setup of the previous section. Let
G^{\prime}
be a connected linear algebraic group acting on a smooth connected algebraic variety
Y
. Denote by
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
the Lie algebra of
G^{\prime}
and by
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
its universal enveloping algebra. Every element
\xi
of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
induces a vector field
Z_{Y}(\xi)\in\Gamma(Y,\Theta_{Y})
by
4.1
, and this map extends to a homomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules

Z_{Y}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Theta_{Y}

via
Z_{Y}(f\otimes\xi)=fZ_{Y}(\xi)
for
f\in\mathcal{O}_{Y}
,
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
.

Definition 4.14
.

Given the
G^{\prime}
-variety
Y
, we define

\mathcal{A}_{Y}:=\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}),

which has the structure of an associative
\mathds{C}
-algebra with multiplication given by

(f_{1}\otimes\xi_{1})\cdot(f_{2}\otimes\xi_{2})=f_{1}f_{2}\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}+f_{1}Z_{Y}(\xi_{1})(f_{2})\otimes\xi_{2}.

The
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module homomorphism
Z_{Y}
extends to a homomorphism of associative
\mathds{C}
-algebras

\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}.

For any left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module
\mathcal{M}
, we may consider the left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module obtained by scalar extension

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathcal{M}.

On the other hand, note that the homomorphism
\tilde{Z}_{Y}
induces a forgetful functor from the category of left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules to the category of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules.

The associative algebra
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
is the universal enveloping algebra of the Lie algebroid
(\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime},\,Z_{Y})
on
Y
, see
[
BB93
, 1.8.4.Example]
. This is the reason why, in many ways, modules over
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
behave similarly to modules over the algebra
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
(which can be viewed as the universal enveloping algebra of the Lie algebroid
\Theta_{Y}
). For example, the tensor product of two left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
is again naturally a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module, while the tensor product of a left and a right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
naturally becomes a right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module. Applying basic results on modules over universal enveloping algebras of Lie algebroids
[
CMNM05
, Appendice]
to
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
, we obtain the following elementary properties:

Lemma 4.15

(
[
CMNM05
, Théorème A.6 and Corollaire A.2]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module. Let
\mathcal{N}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
) be a left (resp. right)
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module. Then there are natural isomorphisms

1.

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{N})\cong(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathcal{M})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{N}
as left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules,

2.

(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{N}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}\cong(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathcal{M})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
as right
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules.

Here, on the left hand sides,
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
are considered as
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules via
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
.

From now on, we will only be interested in the case that
G^{\prime}
acts transitively on
Y
. In this case, the
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module homomorphism
Z_{Y}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Theta_{Y}
is surjective, hence the same is true for
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
, so

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\cong\mathcal{A}_{Y}/\ker\tilde{Z}_{Y}.

We observe that the kernel of
\tilde{Z}_{Y}
(which is a two-sided ideal in
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
) is generated as a left ideal in
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
by the kernel of
Z_{Y}
:

Lemma 4.16
.

If
G^{\prime}
acts transitively on
Y
, then

\ker\big(\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\big)=\mathcal{A}_{Y}\cdot\ker\big(Z_{Y}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Theta_{Y}\big).

Proof.

We check the claim locally. For this, let
p\in Y
be an arbitrary point and let
U\subseteq Y
be an open neighborhood of
p
admitting a local coordinate system
(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
, so that
\Theta_{U}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{U}\partial_{x_{i}}
. We claim that by further shrinking the open set
U
, we may choose
an appropriate
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-basis
\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{m}
of the free
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
such that the surjective homomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-modules

{(Z_{Y})_{|{U}}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Theta_{U}

is given by

\theta_{i}\mapsto\begin{cases}\partial_{x_{i}}&\text{if }i\leq n\\
0&\text{if }i>n.\end{cases}

Indeed,
{(Z_{Y})_{|{U}}}
is a surjective homomorphism of free
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-modules of finite rank and we may represent it by an
n\times m
-matrix
A
(with
m\geq n
) by choosing
any

\mathcal{O}_{U}
-basis of
\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. By surjectivity of
Z_{Y}
, some
n\times n
-minor of
A
does not vanish at the point
p
. After permuting the chosen
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-basis of
\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
, we may assume that the non-vanishing set
V\subseteq U
of the minor given by the first
n
columns is an open neighborhood of
p
. Writing

A=(A_{1}\,|\,A_{2})\qquad\text{with }A_{1}\in\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n,\mathcal{O}_{U}),A_{2}\in\operatorname{Mat}(n\times(m-n),\mathcal{O}_{U}),

we have
A_{1}\in\GL(n,\mathcal{O}_{V})
. Changing the
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-basis on
{(\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})_{|{V}}}=\mathcal{O}_{V}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
corresponds to right-multiplying
A
with an element of
\GL(m,\mathcal{O}_{V})
. Then

\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&A_{2}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}^{-1}&-A_{1}^{-1}A_{2}\\
0&\Id_{m-n}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\Id_{n}&0\end{pmatrix}

shows that a choice of
\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{m}
as desired exists.

Now, every section of
\mathcal{A}_{U}
can be expressed as a sum of elements of the form
f\theta_{1}^{a_{1}}\theta_{2}^{a_{2}}\dots\theta_{m}^{a_{m}}
with
f\in\mathcal{O}_{U}
,
a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\in\mathds{N}
, each of which gets mapped under
{(\tilde{Z}_{Y})_{|{U}}}
to

f\theta_{1}^{a_{1}}\theta_{2}^{a_{2}}\dots\theta_{m}^{a_{m}}\mapsto\begin{cases}f\partial_{x_{1}}^{a_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}^{a_{2}}\dots\partial_{x_{n}}^{a_{n}}&\text{if }a_{n+1}=\dots=a_{m}=0,\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

From this, we can see that every section of
\mathcal{A}_{U}
getting mapped to zero under
{(\tilde{Z}_{Y})_{|{U}}}
is an element of

\mathcal{A}_{U}\cdot\{\theta_{n+1},\dots,\theta_{m}\}=\mathcal{A}_{U}\cdot\ker({(Z_{Y})_{|{U}}}).\qed

4.16
is in fact a special case of a more general fact about Lie algebroids: If
\varphi\colon\mathscr{F}_{1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{2}
is a surjective homomorphism of two locally free Lie algebroids of finite rank on the same variety
Y
, then the kernel of the induced homomorphism of universal enveloping algebras
\tilde{\varphi}\colon\mathcal{U}(\mathscr{F}_{1})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathscr{F}_{2})
is generated by
\ker\varphi
as a left
\mathcal{U}(\mathscr{F}_{1})
-ideal. A similar proof to the above carries over.

Equivariant line bundles as
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules:

If
E\to Y
is a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle and we denote by
\mathscr{E}
its sheaf of sections, then for every open subset
U\subseteq Y
, the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
acts on
\Gamma(U,\mathscr{E})
. This makes
\mathscr{E}
a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module. We will be particularly interested in the left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
arising from this.

Remark 4.17
.

If
U\subseteq Y
is an open subset not invariant under
G^{\prime}
, then
G^{\prime}
does not act on
U
. Yet, we still get
Z_{U}\colon\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Theta_{U}
, allowing us to define
\mathcal{A}_{U}
.
While
{\mathscr{E}_{|{U}}}
is not
G^{\prime}
-equivariant, it still is a left
\mathcal{A}_{U}
-module, and we may consider
\mathscr{D}_{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{U}}{\mathscr{E}_{|{U}}}
. This suggests a generalized viewpoint, where we replace the
G^{\prime}
-action on
Y
by a
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
-action on
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, and replace
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles with line bundles carrying a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure.

Next, we examine when equivariant line bundles give rise to non-zero
\mathscr{D}
-modules.

Proposition 4.18
.

Assume
G^{\prime}
acts transitively on
Y
. Let
\mathscr{E}
be a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle on
Y
. Then the following are equivalent:

1.

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}\neq 0
,

2.

\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
is an isomorphism of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules,

3.

\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}
is an isomorphism of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules for some
k\in\mathds{Z}_{>0}
,

4.

\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}
is an isomorphism of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules for all
k\in\mathds{Z}_{>0}
.

Proof.

First, we show that the first two items are equivalent:
By transitivity of the group action,
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
is surjective, hence the natural homomorphism of
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules
\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
is also surjective. Since the support of
\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
is a
G^{\prime}
-invariant subset of
Y
, by transitivity we must either have
\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}=0
or
\textup{Supp}(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E})=Y
. Since
\mathscr{E}
is a line bundle on
Y
, the only quotient of the
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{E}
with support equal to
Y
is
\mathscr{E}
itself. This shows
Item

1

\Leftrightarrow

Item

2
.

The implication
Item

4

\Rightarrow

Item

3
is trivial. To show the implication
Item

2

\Rightarrow

Item

4
, we assume for contradiction that there is some
k\geq 2
for which the claim does not hold and assume
k
to be minimal. Applying
4.15
.
Item

1
to
\mathcal{M}:=\mathscr{E}^{\otimes(k-1)}
and
\mathcal{N}:=\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
gives:

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}\cong(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}^{\otimes(k-1)})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E})\cong\mathscr{E}^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}

as left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules (by minimality of
k
). This is a contradiction to the choice of
k
.

It remains to show the implication
Item

3

\Rightarrow

Item

2
. Consider the two-sided ideal

\mathcal{I}:=\ker\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{Y}}}\right)

of
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
.
Note that the natural homomorphism
\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules is an isomorphism if and only if
\mathcal{I}
annihilates
\mathscr{E}
. Using
4.16
, it suffices to prove that
\mathscr{E}
is annihilated by
\ker(Z_{Y})
. Let
s\in\Gamma(U,\mathscr{E})
be a non-zero local section of
\mathscr{E}
and let
P\in\Gamma(U,\ker(Z_{Y}))\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
.
By assumption
Item

3
, we have
\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}\cong\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}
as left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules for some
k\geq 1
, meaning that
\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}
is annihilated by
\mathcal{I}
. In particular, the local section
s^{k}\in\Gamma(U,\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k})
is annihilated by
P
, so
P\cdot s^{k}=0
. On the other hand, we have

P\cdot s^{k}=ks^{k-1}(P\cdot s).

Since
Y
is an irreducible variety, we deduce that
P\cdot s=0
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.19
.

Assume
G^{\prime}
acts transitively on
Y
. Let
\mathscr{E}
be a torsion element of the equivariant Picard group
\Pic^{G^{\prime}}(Y)
, i.e.,
\mathscr{E}^{\otimes k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
as equivariant line bundles for some
k\in\mathds{Z}_{>0}
. Then
the natural homomorphism

\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}

of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By
4.18
, it suffices to consider the case that
\mathscr{E}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}
as equivariant line bundles. The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
acts trivially on the
1
-section of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, hence

\mathscr{E}\cong\mathcal{A}_{Y}/\mathcal{A}_{Y}(\xi\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})

as left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules. Tensoring with
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
over
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
gives

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}\cong\mathscr{D}_{Y}/\mathscr{D}_{Y}(Z_{Y}(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})=\mathscr{D}_{Y}/\mathscr{D}_{Y}\Theta_{Y}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cong\mathscr{E}.

Here, we use that the vector fields
Z_{Y}(\xi)
for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
generate the tangent bundle
\Theta_{Y}
, as the action of
G^{\prime}
on
Y
is transitive.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

Note from the proof above that the equivalences of 2., 3. and 4. in
4.18
hold more generally for any line bundle
\mathscr{E}
with a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure, not necessarily arising from
G^{\prime}
-equivariant structure on
\mathscr{E}
. The equivalence with 1. moreover holds whenever
\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{Y}}\mathscr{E}
is known to have
G^{\prime}
-invariant support (as will be the case for example if we know that some positive power of
\mathscr{E}
underlies a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle, or if
\mathscr{E}
is a twist of a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle by a Lie algebra homomorphism as we will consider in
Section

4.3
).

4.19
shows in particular that
\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}\neq 0
for
G^{\prime}
-equivariant torsion line bundles. Under certain assumptions on
Y
, the converse is also true:

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
G^{\prime}
act transitively on
Y
and assume that there is an open cover
Y=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
such that for each
i\in I
, there is a subgroup
N_{i}
of
G
acting freely and transitively on
U_{i}
. Then

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}\neq 0\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\mathscr{E}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}\text{ as $G^{\prime}$-equivariant line bundles}.

We remark that under the assumptions on
Y
in
4.21
, there are no non-trivial equivariant torsion line bundles on
Y
.

Proof.

One implication is given by
4.19
. For the converse, we assume that
\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E}\neq 0
. Since
\mathscr{E}
is
G^{\prime}
-equivariant, the support of this
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module is a non-empty
G^{\prime}
-invariant subset of
Y
, hence (by transitivity of the group action)

\textup{Supp}(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E})=Y.

(12)

In particular, the restriction to
U_{i}
is a non-zero
\mathscr{D}_{U_{i}}
-module for each
i\in I
.

Denote by
E^{*}
the complement of the zero section of
E=\Tot(\mathscr{E})\xrightarrow{\pi}Y
. For
i\in I
, the choice of a point
w_{i}\in E^{*}
such that
p_{i}:=\pi(w_{i})\in U_{i}
determines a local section
s_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathscr{E})
geometrically given by

\displaystyle s_{i}\colon\quad U_{i}\ \xrightarrow{\cong}\

\displaystyle N_{i}\ \to\ \pi^{-1}(U_{i})

\displaystyle g\cdot p_{i}\ \mapsfrom\

\displaystyle g\quad\mapsto\ g\cdot w_{i}.

Here, we use that
N_{i}\to U_{i}
,
g\mapsto g\cdot p_{i}
is an isomorphism, since
N_{i}
is assumed to act freely and transitively on
U_{i}
. Since
E^{*}
is invariant under the action of
G^{\prime}
on
E
, the local section
s_{i}
does not vanish on
U_{i}
, hence
{\mathscr{E}_{|{U_{i}}}}=\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}s_{i}
.

By definition,
s_{i}
is an
N_{i}
-invariant section of
{\mathscr{E}_{|{U_{i}}}}
, hence
\xi\cdot s_{i}=0
holds for all
\xi\in\Lie(N_{i})=:\mathfrak{n}_{i}
. Since
N_{i}
acts transitively on
U_{i}
, the
\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}
-module homomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}\otimes\mathfrak{n}_{i}\to\Theta_{U_{i}}
is surjective, so from the above we may deduce that
\Theta_{U_{i}}
annihilates the cyclic
\mathscr{D}_{U_{i}}
-module
{(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E})_{|{U_{i}}}}
generated by
1\otimes s_{i}
.

Take any
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. Then
\xi\cdot s_{i}=f\cdot s_{i}
for some
f\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}})
. But then
f
annihilates
{(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{E})_{|{U_{i}}}}
, as
f\cdot(1\otimes s_{i})=1\otimes(\xi\cdot s_{i})={Z_{Y}(\xi)_{|{U_{i}}}}\cdot(1\otimes s_{i})=0
. Because of (
12
), this forces

\xi\cdot s_{i}=0\qquad\text{for all }\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}.

On
U_{ij}:=U_{i}\cap U_{j}
for
i,j\in I
, the non-vanishing local sections
s_{i}
and
s_{j}
only differ by an invertible function:

{(s_{i})_{|{U_{ij}}}}=\alpha_{ij}{(s_{j})_{|{U_{ij}}}},\qquad\alpha_{ij}\in\Gamma(U_{ij},\mathcal{O}_{U_{ij}}^{\times}).

Since

0=\xi\cdot{(s_{i})_{|{U_{ij}}}}=\xi\cdot(\alpha_{ij}{(s_{j})_{|{U_{ij}}}})={Z_{Y}(\xi)_{|{U_{ij}}}}(\alpha_{ij}){(s_{j})_{|{U_{ij}}}}+\alpha_{ij}(\xi\cdot{(s_{j})_{|{U_{ij}}}})={Z_{Y}(\xi)_{|{U_{ij}}}}(\alpha_{ij}){(s_{j})_{|{U_{ij}}}},

we see that
\alpha_{ij}=0
is annihilated by all vector fields on
U_{ij}
(since
\Theta_{Y}
is globally generated by the image of
Z_{Y}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\Theta_{Y}
). Therefore,
\alpha_{ij}\in\mathds{C}^{*}
.

We may now fix some
k\in I
and define non-vanishing sections

\tilde{s}_{i}:=\alpha_{ki}^{-1}s_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathscr{E})\qquad\text{for all }i\in I

which are still annihilated by the action of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
.
Then
\tilde{s}_{i}
and
\tilde{s}_{j}
agree on
U_{ij}
for all
i,j\in I
, so they glue to a global non-vanishing section
\tilde{s}\in\Gamma(Y,\mathscr{E})
annihilated by
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. This section defines an isomorphism
\mathscr{E}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
of left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules and hence of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles.
∎

4.3
Twist by characters and non-vanishing of tautological systems

Next we relate the construction from the previous section to the
\mathscr{D}
-modules
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
from
4.6
. Recall from
4.11
that these
\mathscr{D}
-modules describe restrictions of Fourier-transformed tautological systems and hence we obtain in
4.28
below a non-vanishing result for tautological systems
\tau(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)
based on the non-vanishing of
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
.

To start with, we need to consider twists of equivariant line bundles by characters:

Definition 4.22
.

Let
\chi\colon G^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}^{*}
be a character. We define a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\chi\}
on
Y
by equipping the trivial line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
with a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant structure such that the action of
G^{\prime}
on
\Tot(\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\chi\})=\mathds{C}\times Y
is given by
g\cdot(\lambda,y)=(\chi(g)\lambda,\,g\cdot y)
.

For any
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle
\mathscr{E}
, consider the
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle

\mathscr{E}\{\chi\}:=\mathscr{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\chi\},

which has the same underlying
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module, but a different equivariant structure.

One easily checks that

\operatorname{Hom}(G^{\prime},\mathds{C}^{*})\to\Pic^{G^{\prime}}(Y)
,
\chi\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\chi\}
is a group homomorphism.

Remark 4.23
.

For a given equivariant line bundle
\mathscr{E}
whose
G^{\prime}
-action is given on
E:=\Tot(\mathscr{E})
as
\varphi\colon G^{\prime}\times E\to E
, the
G^{\prime}
-action on
\Tot(\mathscr{E}\{\chi\})=E
is given by

G^{\prime}\times E\to E,\qquad(g,e)\mapsto\mu\big(\chi(g),\varphi(g,e)\big),

where
\mu\colon\mathds{C}^{*}\times E\to E
denotes the natural
\mathds{C}^{*}
-action on
E
by scaling fibers.

We have seen before that every
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle on
Y
is a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module, so for every character
\chi\colon G^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}^{*}
, we get the left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module

\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\chi\}\cong\mathcal{A}_{Y}/\mathcal{A}_{Y}(\xi-\differential\chi(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}),

where
\differential\chi\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
is the Lie algebra homomorphism induced by
\chi
.

Note that the left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle
\mathscr{E}
results just from the infinitesimal action of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
on local sections of
\mathscr{E}
. Therefore, it is natural to make the following more general definition:

Definition 4.24
.

For any Lie algebra homomorphism
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
, we define the left
\mathscr{A}_{Y}
-module

\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\beta\}:=\mathcal{A}_{Y}/\mathcal{A}_{Y}(\xi-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}).

If
\mathscr{E}
is a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module, then denote by
\mathscr{E}\{\beta\}
the left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\beta\}
.

This may in general not be a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle. Note that
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\chi\}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\differential\chi\}
as
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules for
\chi\colon G^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}^{*}
inducing
\differential\chi\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
. Similarly to before, given a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{E}
, we denote by
\mathscr{E}\{\beta\}
the left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module
\mathscr{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\beta\}
. If
\mathscr{E}
is a line bundle with a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure, we denote
\mathscr{(}\mathscr{E}\{\beta\})^{\vee}:=\mathscr{E}^{\vee}\{-\beta\}
.

Recall from
4.6
that for any Lie algebra homomorphism
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
on a smooth connected
G^{\prime}
-variety
Y
, we defined the left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module

\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}:=\omega_{Y}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}/(Z_{Y}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{Y}.

Our next aim is to show the following result describing this
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module as arising from a
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
-module structure on the anticanonical bundle on
Y
:

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
be a Lie algebra homomorphism. Considering
\omega_{Y}
with its natural
G^{\prime}
-equivariant structure, there is an isomorphism of left
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules

\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\cong\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}.

For the proof of
4.25
, we need some technical remarks on left-right transforms of
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules that we carry out first:

The line bundle
\alpha_{Y}:=\bigwedge^{\dim G^{\prime}}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})^{\vee}
on
Y
has the structure of a right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module which is given by the negated Lie derivative: A Lie algebra element
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
acts on an alternating form
\omega
by mapping it to the alternating form
\omega\cdot\xi
given by

(\omega\cdot\xi)(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{m})=-Z_{Y}(\xi)(\omega(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{m}))+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\omega(\theta_{1},\dots,[\xi,\theta_{i}],\dots,\theta_{m})

for any
\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{m}\in\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathds{C}}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. This defines transformations between left and right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules giving rise to an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mod}(\mathcal{A}_{Y})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt\cong\hskip 16.38895pt}\operatorname{Mod}(\mathcal{A}_{Y}^{\text{op}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\qquad\longmapsto\qquad\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{M},

\displaystyle\alpha_{Y}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\longmapsfrom\qquad\mathcal{M}^{\prime}.

Remark 4.26
.

If
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m}
form a
\mathds{C}
-basis of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
, then

\alpha_{Y}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}\,\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*}

The right action on
\alpha_{Y}
is given by

(f\,\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*})\cdot\xi=\big(\!\trace(\ad(\xi))-Z_{Y}(\xi)(f)\big)\,\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*}\qquad\text{for }\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}.

In general, if
\mathcal{M}
is a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module, then the right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on
\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{M}
is given by

(\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*}\otimes s)\cdot\xi=\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*}\otimes\big(\!\trace(\ad(\xi))-\xi\big)\cdot s\quad\text{for }\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime},s\in\mathcal{M}.

The canonical bundle
\omega_{Y}
on
Y
is a right
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module and hence, via
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
, it also has the structure of a right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module. On the other hand, the action of
G^{\prime}
on
Y
extends naturally to an action on the tangent bundle on
Y
, so
\omega_{Y}=\bigwedge^{\dim Y}\Theta_{Y}^{\vee}
is naturally a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle, which induces a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure. The next lemma states that these left and right module structures on
\omega_{Y}
relate to each other via the transformation above:

Lemma 4.27
.

Let
\delta:=\trace\circ\ad\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
and let
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m}
form a
\mathds{C}
-basis of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. There is an isomorphism of right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules

\displaystyle\omega_{Y}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}\{\delta\}

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*}\otimes s,

where on the left hand side,
\omega_{Y}
is endowed with its right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure induced from the homomorphism
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
, and on the right hand side, we consider
\omega_{Y}
with its left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure by viewing it as a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle.

Proof.

Denote
a:=\xi_{1}^{*}\wedge\dots\wedge\xi_{m}^{*}\in\Gamma(Y,\alpha_{Y})
and recall that
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m}
are a
\mathds{C}
-basis of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. Since
a
is a non-vanishing global section of the line bundle
\alpha_{Y}
, the homomorphism
\omega_{Y}\to\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta\}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}
,
s\mapsto a\otimes 1\otimes s
is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules, hence it suffices to show:

(a\otimes 1\otimes s)\cdot\xi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}a\otimes 1\otimes(s\cdot\xi)

for
s\in\omega_{Y}
,
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
.

The right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on
\omega_{Y}
(inherited from the right
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module structure) is given by

(s\cdot\xi)(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{m})=-Z_{Y}(\xi)(s(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n}))+\sum_{i=1}^{m}s(\theta_{1},\dots,[Z_{Y}(\xi),\theta_{i}],\dots,\theta_{n})

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
,
s\in\omega_{Y}
,
\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n}\in\Theta_{Y}
. On the other hand, the right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module
\alpha_{Y}
satisfies
a\cdot\xi=\delta(\xi)a
, so the right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on
\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta\}
satisfies

(a\otimes 1)\cdot\xi=0.

The left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on
\omega_{Y}
results from the left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on the
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundle
\Theta_{Y}
given by

\xi\cdot\theta=[Z_{Y}(\xi),\theta]\qquad\text{for all }\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}

The induced left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on
\bigwedge^{n}\Theta_{Y}
is given by

\xi\cdot(\theta_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\theta_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge[Z_{Y}(\xi),\theta_{i}]\wedge\dots\wedge\theta_{n}

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. Passing to the dual line bundle
\omega_{Y}
, we get

\displaystyle(\xi\cdot s)(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n})

\displaystyle=Z_{Y}(\xi)(s(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n}))-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s(\theta_{1},\dots,[Z_{Y}(\xi),\theta_{i}],\dots,\theta_{n})

\displaystyle=-(s\cdot\xi)(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{n})\qquad\text{for all }\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}.

The right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure on
\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta\}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}
resulting from this satisfies

(a\otimes 1\otimes s)\cdot\xi=((a\otimes 1)\cdot\xi)\otimes s-(a\otimes 1)\otimes(\xi\cdot s)=a\otimes 1\otimes(s\cdot\xi)

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
.
∎

We can now turn to the proof of the description of
\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}
via the (twisted) equivariant
anti-canonical bundle:

Proof of
4.25
.

Equivalently to the claim, we may show that there is an isomorphism between the corresponding right
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules

\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\cong}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}/(Z_{Y}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{Y}.

By
4.15
.
Item

2
, we have an isomorphism of right
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules

\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\big)\cong(\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y},

where, on the right hand side, the first occurrence of
\omega_{Y}
is equipped with the right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module structure inherited from its right
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-module structure. Combining this with
4.27
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\big)

\displaystyle\cong(\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}\{\delta\}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}

\displaystyle\cong(\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta-\beta\})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}

where
\delta:=\trace\circ\ad\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
and
\omega_{Y}
is now considered as a left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module via its natural structure as a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle. Since
\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
as
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles (and therefore also as left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-module), we conclude:

\omega_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\omega_{Y}^{\vee}\{\beta\}\big)\cong(\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta-\beta\})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}\mathscr{D}_{Y}.

Recall that
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta-\beta\}\cong\mathcal{A}_{Y}/\mathcal{A}_{Y}(\xi-(\delta-\beta)(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
, so by
4.26
, we have

\alpha_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\{\delta-\beta\}\cong\mathcal{A}_{Y}/(\xi-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathcal{A}_{Y}

as right
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules. Tensoring with
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
over
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
by means of the homomorphism
\tilde{Z}_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}_{Y}\to\mathscr{D}_{Y}
yields the claimed result.
∎

By using all the constructions and results of this section, we get the following non-vanishing theorem for tautological systems:

Theorem 4.28
.

Let
\rho\colon G^{\prime}\to\GL(V)
be a finite-dimensional rational representation. Let
Y\subseteq V
be a
G^{\prime}
-orbit and let
\overline{Y}
be its closure. Let
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
be a Lie algebra homomorphism. If
(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\otimes k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
for some
k\in\mathds{Z}
as left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules,
then
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)\neq 0
, and hence also
\tau(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)\neq 0
.

Proof.

By
4.11
, we have
i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{Y}^{\beta}\cong{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)_{|{U}}}
, where
i
denotes the closed embedding of
Y
into
U:=V\setminus\partial Y
for
\partial Y:=\overline{Y}\setminus Y
. With
4.25
, we conclude that

{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\overline{Y},\beta)_{|{U}}}\cong i_{+}\big(\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\big)

as left
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-modules. To show that the right hand side is non-zero, it suffices to see that we have
\mathscr{D}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\neq 0
.
But this follows from
4.19
respectively
4.20
, because we assumed that
(\omega_{Y}\{\beta\})^{\otimes k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
as left
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
-modules.
∎

4.4
Application to projective homogeneous spaces

We now apply the previous results to the following setup: Consider a smooth projective variety
X
with a transitive action of a reductive connected linear algebraic group
G
(i.e.,
X
is a
homogeneous space
). Let
L\to X
be a
G
-equivariant line bundle on
X
with sheaf of sections
\mathscr{L}
. We consider
G^{\prime}:=G\times\mathds{C}^{*}
and denote the Lie algebras involved by
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}:=\Lie(G^{\prime})
,
\mathfrak{g}:=\Lie(G)
and
\Lie(\mathds{C}^{*})=\mathds{C}\mathbf{e}
, so

\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathds{C}\mathbf{e}.

We view
\mathscr{L}
as a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
by letting the
\mathds{C}^{*}
-factor of
G^{\prime}
act trivially on
X
and by inverse scaling on the fibers of
L\to X
. Note that then
G^{\prime}
acts transitively on
L^{*}
.
Denote by
L^{*}\subseteq L
the complement of its zero section. The morphisms to
X
are denoted
\pi^{L}\colon L\to X
and
\pi^{L^{*}}\colon L^{*}\to X
.

Lemma 4.29
.

Every point of
X
admits an open neighborhood on which a subgroup of
G
acts freely and transitively. The same holds for the action of
G^{\prime}
on
L^{*}
.

Proof.

For any point
p\in X
, the stabilizer
P:=\{g\in G\mid g\cdot p=p\}
describes the variety as a quotient:

G/P\xrightarrow{\cong}X,\qquad gP\mapsto g\cdot p.

Since
X
is projective, the subgroup
P\subseteq G
is parabolic. Let
N^{-}\subseteq G
be the unipotent radical of the opposite parabolic subgroup to
P
in
G
. Then
N^{-}\cap P=1
, which shows that
N^{-}
acts freely and transitively on the
N^{-}
-orbit
N^{-}\cdot p
. On the other hand, we have
\Lie(N^{-})\oplus\Lie(P)=\mathfrak{g}
as
\mathds{C}
-vector spaces, so
N^{-}\cdot p
is of dimension
\dim G-\dim P=\dim X
, hence it is an open neighborhood of
p
in
X
.

For the
G^{\prime}
-action on
L^{*}
, take any point
w\in L^{*}
and consider
p=\pi^{L^{*}}(w)
in the argument above. Then
\pi^{L^{*},-1}(U)
is an open neighborhood of
w
in
L^{*}
on which
\mathds{C}^{*}\times N^{-}\subseteq G^{\prime}
acts transitively and freely.
∎

In the following, we remark that the assumptions on
X
guarantee that we are in the setup of
Section

2
.

Remark 4.30
.

Projective homogeneous spaces
X\cong G/P
are smooth Fano variety (we recall a short representation-theoretic argument in
5.14
below). As such, it has the property that the underlying complex manifold
X^{\mathrm{an}}
is simply-connected (see e.g.
[
Deb01
, Corollary 4.29]
). In particular, we may apply
2.1
to
X^{\mathrm{an}}
to get
k\in\mathds{Z}_{>0}
with
\pi_{1}(L^{*,\mathrm{an}})\cong\mathds{Z}/k\mathds{Z}
and we may consider the
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-modules
\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}
for
\ell\in\mathds{Z}
as in
2.2
.

Additionally, the Fano property of
X
implies
H^{i}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
for all
i>0
by the Kodaira vanishing theorem, hence in particular
\Pic(X)=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})\cong H^{2}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathds{Z})
. In algebraic terms, the integer
k
in the statement of
2.1
is therefore the largest positive integer such that
\mathscr{L}
admits a
k
-th root in the Picard group. Moreover,
\Pic(X)\cong H^{2}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathds{Z})
has no torsion, as observed in the proof of
2.1
.

Lemma 4.31
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
. Then

\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\mathcal{M}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}\text{ for some $r\in\mathds{Z}$},

where both sides are isomorphisms of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles.

Proof.

For the implication “
\Leftarrow
”, it suffices to consider the case
r=1
. Note that the
G^{\prime}
-equivariant structure on
\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathscr{L}
corresponds to the diagonal
G^{\prime}
-action on
\Tot(\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathscr{L})=L^{*}\times_{X}L\xrightarrow{pr_{1}}L^{*}
. Note further that the map

s\colon L^{*}\xrightarrow{\Delta}L^{*}\times_{X}L^{*}\hookrightarrow L^{*}\times_{X}L

is a
G^{\prime}
-invariant global section of the line bundle
\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathscr{L}
that vanishes nowhere on
L^{*}
. Then

\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}\to\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathscr{L},\qquad 1\mapsto s

is an isomorphism of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles.

To show the implication “
\Rightarrow
”, let
\mathcal{M}
be a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
with
\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}
. This means that there is a
G^{\prime}
-invariant global section of
\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathcal{M}
which we may view as a morphism

s\colon L^{*}\to\Tot(\pi^{L^{*},*}\mathcal{M})=L^{*}\times_{X}M,

where
M=\Tot(\mathcal{M})
and where
s
is the identity on the first component. Since the section is non-vanishing, it is therefore given by
s=\id_{L^{*}}\times\varphi
for some morphism

\varphi\colon L^{*}\to M^{*}

over
X
. The
G^{\prime}
-invariance of the section
s
translates to
G^{\prime}
-equivariance of the morphism
\varphi
. Recall that the
\mathds{C}^{*}
-factor of
G^{\prime}=G\times\mathds{C}^{*}
acts trivially on
X
and by inverse scaling on the fibers of
\pi^{L}\colon L\to X
. Notice that on
M
, the
\mathds{C}^{*}
-action must also be given fiberwise, so there exists some
r\in\mathds{Z}
such that
\mathds{C}^{*}\subseteq G^{\prime}
acts on fibers of
\pi^{M}\colon M\to X
by scaling with
(-r)
-th powers. In particular, the
\mathds{C}^{*}
-equivariance of
\varphi\colon L^{*}\to M^{*}
implies the following fiberwise description: Over
p\in X
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{p}\colon L_{p}^{*}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathds{C}^{*}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{r}}\mathds{C}^{*}\xrightarrow{\cong}M_{p}^{*}

\displaystyle\lambda w\ \mapsfrom\ \lambda\

\displaystyle\phantom{\xrightarrow{\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{r}}{}}\ \ \mu\ \mapsto\ \mu\varphi(w)

for any choice of
w\in L_{p}^{*}
.
From this, we can conclude
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}
, for instance as follows: Take an open cover
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
trivializing
\mathscr{L}
as
{\mathscr{L}_{|{U_{i}}}}=\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}s_{i}
for some choice of non-vanishing local sections
s_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathscr{L})
. On
U_{ij}:=U_{i}\cap U_{j}
, we have
s_{i}=\alpha_{ij}s_{j}
for some
\alpha_{ij}\in\Gamma(U_{ij},\mathcal{O}_{U_{ij}}^{\times})
and the collection
(\alpha_{ij})_{i,j\in I}
forms a Čech cocycle whose class in
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})\cong\Pic(X)
defines the isomorphism class of
\mathscr{L}
. Viewing the sections
s_{i}
geometrically as morphisms
U_{i}\to L^{*}
, we may compose them with
\varphi
to get non-vanishing local sections
\varphi\circ s_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathcal{M})
. On
U_{ij}
, we then have
\varphi\circ s_{i}=(\alpha_{ij}^{r})(\varphi\circ s_{j})
since
\varphi
is given on fibers of
X
by taking
r
-th powers. This shows that the class of
\mathcal{M}
in the Picard group of
X
is the class of the Čech cocycle
(\alpha_{ij}^{r})_{i,j\in I}
in
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})\cong\Pic(X)
. On the other hand, the same is true for the line bundle
\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}
(as can e.g. be seen in the same way, using the morphism
L^{*}\to L^{\otimes r,*}
given by fiberwise
r
-th powers). Hence,
\mathcal{M}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}
and the
G^{\prime}
-equivariance of this isomorphism follows from the
G^{\prime}
-equivariance of
\varphi
.
∎

Lemma 4.32
.

There is an isomorphism

\omega_{L}\cong\pi^{L,*}\omega_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\pi^{L,*}\mathscr{L}^{\vee}

of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles on
L
. In particular,

\omega_{L^{*}}\cong\pi^{L^{*},*}\omega_{X}

as
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles on
L^{*}
.

Proof.

The second claim follows directly from the first claim by pulling back the line bundles to
L^{*}
and using
4.31
. Hence, it suffices to prove the formula for
\omega_{L}
.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundle on
X
and put
F:=\textup{Tot}(\mathscr{F})
with projection
\pi^{F}\colon F\rightarrow X
.
The variety
F
is then equipped with a
G^{\prime}
-action. We first claim that there is an isomorphism

\Theta_{F/X}\cong\pi^{F,*}\mathscr{F}.

of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundles on
F
,
and a corresponding isomorphism
\Omega^{1}_{F/X}\cong\pi^{F,*}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
of dual vector bundles. Namely, any section
s\in\Gamma(U,\mathcal{F})
can be
considered as an element
s\in\Gamma(U,{\mathcal{H}\!}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee},\mathcal{O}_{X}))
,
and it extends via the Leibniz rule as a section of

\Gamma(U,{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}({\mathcal{S}\!}ym_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee}))
. This yields a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}({\mathcal{S}\!}ym_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee}))={\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\pi^{F}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{F}).

It is also injective,
since for any
s\neq 0
, there is some section of
\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
that
is not killed by
s
, so that
s
is not the zero derivation in
{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\pi^{F}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{F})
. Since both
\mathscr{F}
and
{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\pi^{F}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{F})

are locally free of the same rank, it follows that the cokernel of the inclusion

\mathscr{F}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\pi^{F}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{F})
, if not zero, must be a torsion sheaf on
X
, but this is impossible since this map is equivariant, and so is its cokernel.
We conclude that there is an isomorphism

\mathscr{F}\cong{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\pi^{F}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{F})
of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundles on
X
. Applying the functor
\pi^{F,*}
then
yields an isomorphism

\pi^{F,*}\mathscr{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi^{F,*}{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\pi^{F}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\star)}}{{\cong}}{\mathscr{D}\!}er_{\pi^{F,-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{O}_{F})\cong\Theta_{F/X},

of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant bundles on
F
, as required. Notice that the isomorphism
(\star)
in the above displayed formula holds since the map
\pi^{F}
is affine.

We apply this to the special case
F=L
, i.e.,
\rk(\mathscr{F})=1
, to obtain the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-isomorphism

\omega_{L/X}\cong\pi^{L,*}\mathscr{L}^{\vee},

(13)

which again is
G^{\prime}
-equivariant.

Consider the cotangent sequence

0\longrightarrow\pi^{L,*}\Omega^{1}_{X}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{L}\longrightarrow\omega_{L/X}\longrightarrow 0,

which, since
\pi^{L}\colon L\rightarrow X
is
G^{\prime}
-equivariant, is an exact sequence of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundles on
L
. Applying
\bigwedge^{\dim(X)+1}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(-)
to this sequence, we get the following isomorphism of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles on
L
:

\omega_{L}\cong\pi^{L,*}\omega_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\omega_{L/X}.

Plugging in the isomorphism from Equation (
13
) yields

\omega_{L}\cong\pi^{L,*}\omega_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\pi^{L,*}\mathscr{L}^{\vee},

as required.

∎

Proposition 4.33
.

Let

\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
be a Lie algebra homomorphism with
{\beta_{|{\mathfrak{g}}}}\equiv 0
. Let
k\in\mathds{Z}_{>0}
be such that
\pi_{1}(L^{*,\mathrm{an}})\cong\mathds{Z}/k\mathds{Z}
(see
Section

2
). Then

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\cong\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}&\text{if $\exists\ell\in\mathds{Z}:\beta(\mathbf{e})=\ell/k$ and $\mathscr{L}^{\otimes\ell}\cong\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}$ as $G$-equivariant line bundles,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

Recall from
4.6
that

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}:=\omega_{L^{*}}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}}\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}/(Z_{L^{*}}(\xi)-\beta(\xi)\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}.

We first assume that
\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\neq 0
. Since
G^{\prime}
acts transitively on
L^{*}
, the vector fields
Z_{L^{*}}(\xi)
, when
\xi
runs through
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
, generate the tangent bundle of
L^{*}
. This implies that
\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-module of rank one, i.e., corresponds to a local system on
L^{*,\mathrm{an}}
.
By the discussion in
Section

2
, we therefore have an isomorphism of
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-modules

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}
for some
\ell\in\mathds{Z}
. Let
U\subseteq X
be a Zariski open affine coordinate set (in the algebraic sense, see, e.g.
[
HTT08
, Definition A.5.2]
) such that
L
trivializes over
U
. Then

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}|{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}^{\beta}\cong\mathscr{D}_{{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}/\mathscr{D}_{{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}(Z_{L^{*}}(\xi)_{|{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}^{T}\mid\xi\in\mathfrak{g})+\mathscr{D}_{{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}(-\partial_{t}t-\beta(\mathbf{e})),

where we denote by
(-)^{T}
the transpose of a differential operator written in the chosen local coordinates.

Now using
2.3
the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}|{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}^{\ell/k}\cong\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}|{\mathds{C}}^{*}\times U}^{\beta}
can be made explicit, and then it follows easily that
\beta(\mathbf{e})-\ell/k
must be an integer, but this yields an isomorphism
\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\beta(\mathbf{e})}
by the remark after
2.2
. In particular, this shows that when
\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\neq 0
, we must have
\beta(\mathbf{e})\in\frac{1}{k}\mathds{Z}
.

In order to show the remaining statements, we therefore assume that
\beta(\mathbf{e})=\ell/k\in\mathds{Q}
for some
\ell\in\mathds{Z}
. By
4.25
, we know

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\cong\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{L^{*}}}(\omega_{L^{*}}\{\beta\})^{\vee}.

Because of
4.29
, we may apply
4.21
to decide when a
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-module of the form
{\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{L^{*}}}\mathscr{E}}
is non-zero. However, notice that this criterion only applies to equivariant line bundles
\mathscr{E}
, while
(\omega_{L^{*}}\{\beta\})^{\vee}
is for non-integral
\beta(\mathbf{e})
only a line bundle with an
\mathcal{A}_{L^{*}}
-module structure. However, the
k
-th tensor power

(\omega_{L^{*}}\{\beta\})^{\otimes(-k)}\cong\omega_{L^{*}}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-k\beta\},

underlies an equivariant line bundle, since
k\beta=\differential\chi_{\ell}
, where
\chi_{\ell}\colon G^{\prime}=G\times\mathds{C}^{*}\to\mathds{C}^{*}
is the character given by
(g,t)\mapsto t^{\ell}
. With
4.18
/
4.20
and
4.21
, we see that

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\neq 0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{L^{*}}}(\omega_{L^{*}}\{\beta\})^{\vee}\neq 0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{L^{*}}}\omega_{L^{*}}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\neq 0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \omega_{L^{*}}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}.

Now
\omega_{L^{*}}\cong\pi^{L^{*},*}\omega_{X}
as
G^{\prime}
-equivariant line bundles by
4.32
and
4.31
. Hence,

\omega_{L^{*}}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\cong\pi^{L^{*},*}\big(\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\big).

By using this, and invoking
4.31
again, we see that

\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\neq 0\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}\text{ as $G^{\prime}$-equivariant line bundles for some $r\in\mathds{Z}$}.

Since the
\mathds{C}^{*}
-factor of
G^{\prime}
acts trivially on
X
, note that the natural
\mathds{C}^{*}
-equivariant structure on
\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}
is also trivial. On the other hand,
\mathds{C}^{*}
acts by inverse scaling on the fibers of
L=\Tot(\mathscr{L})
. Hence, if
\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}\{\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}
holds for some
r\in\mathds{Z}
, we must have
r=\ell
. Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\neq 0\quad

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}\{-\differential\chi_{\ell}\}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes\ell}\text{ as $G^{\prime}$-equivariant line bundles}.

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}\cong\mathscr{L}^{\otimes\ell}\text{ as $G$-equivariant line bundles}.\qed

We now conclude with the final result of this section classifying when Fourier-transformed tautological systems are non-zero away from the origin. We work in the setup stated at the beginning of this section, i.e.,

X
is projective and admits a transitive action by a reductive algebraic group
G
,
L
is
G
-equivariant,

G^{\prime}:={\mathds{C}}^{*}\times G
, and
G^{\prime}
acts on
L
by letting the
{\mathds{C}}^{*}
-factor act by inverse scaling in the fibres.
Moreover, we now assume that
\mathscr{L}
on
X
is very ample. Consider the
G^{\prime}
-representation
\rho\colon G^{\prime}\to\operatorname{GL}(V)
with
V:=H^{0}(X,\mathscr{L})^{\vee}
and the equivariant closed embedding
X\hookrightarrow\mathds{P}V
defined by
|\mathscr{L}|
. Let
\hat{X}\subseteq V
be the affine cone of
X
in
V
.Notice that we have an isomorphism
\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}\cong L^{\vee,*}
, given by identifying
L^{\vee}
with the blow-up of
\hat{X}
at the origin. We write
i
for the closed embedding of
\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}
into
V\setminus\{0\}
. Together with the isomorphism
\textup{inv}\colon L^{*}\to L^{\vee,*}
given by inverting fibers, we obtain a closed embedding
i^{\prime}\colon L^{*}\hookrightarrow V\setminus\{0\}
defined by
i^{\prime}:=i\circ\textup{inv}
, as shown in the following diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\setminus\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{Bl}_{\{0\}}(\hat{X})\cong L^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\vee,*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{L}}
inv
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{L^{\vee}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

(14)

Theorem 4.34
.

Let

\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
be a Lie algebra homomorphism with
{\beta_{|{\mathfrak{g}}}}\equiv 0
. Let
k\in\mathds{Z}_{>0}
be such that
\pi_{1}(L^{*,\mathrm{an}})\cong\mathds{Z}/k\mathds{Z}
(see
Section

2
). Then

{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)_{|{V\setminus\{0\}}}}\cong\begin{cases}i^{\prime}_{+}\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}&\quad\text{if $\exists\ell\in\mathds{Z}:\beta(\mathbf{e})=\ell/k$ and $\mathscr{L}^{\otimes\ell}\cong\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}$ }\\
&\quad\text{as $G$-equivariant line bundles,}\\
\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

This follows directly from the work above by combining
4.11
(applied to
Y=\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}
) and
4.33
.

For convenience, we roughly summarize the main steps that led to the proof of
4.34
:

•

The restriction of
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)
to
V\setminus\{0\}
is supported on
\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}\cong L^{*}
and can be described as
i_{+}\mathscr{N}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}^{\beta}
(
4.11
). Here,
\mathscr{N}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}^{\beta}
arises from a cyclic right
\mathscr{D}
-module constructed from the vector fields induced by the group action (
4.6
).

•

The
\mathscr{D}
-module
\mathscr{N}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}^{\beta}
is alternatively described as
\mathscr{D}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}}(\omega_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\{\beta\})^{\vee}
(
4.25
), where
\mathcal{A}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}=\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\otimes\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})
and
(\omega_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\{\beta\})^{\vee}
is the anticanonical bundle with a
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
-module structure determined by
\beta
.

•

Identifying
\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}
with
L^{*}
, we can argue that
\mathscr{D}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}}(\omega_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\{\beta\})^{\vee}
is non-zero if and only if
\mathscr{L}
is a
\ell/k
-th rational power of
\omega_{X}^{\vee}
and is equipped with a suitable equivariant structure (
4.33
). The geometric reason is that in this case
(\omega_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}\{\beta\})^{\otimes k}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}\setminus\{0\}}
. This statement is based on vanishing results for
\mathscr{D}
-modules constructed from equivariant line bundles in this way (
4.18
,
4.19
and
4.21
).

•

In the cases that
\mathscr{N}_{\hat{X}\setminus 0}^{\beta}
is non-zero, it is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-module of rank
1
. As such, it is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}
(see
Section

2
) and one confirms that
\beta(\mathbf{e})=\ell/k
(see
4.33
). ∎

Remark 4.35
.

If
G
is a semisimple linear algebraic group, then we have
[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]=\mathfrak{g}
. This shows that
{\beta_{|{\mathfrak{g}}}}\equiv 0
holds for every Lie algebra homomorphism
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
, so this condition in
4.34
is always fulfilled in this case.

Remark 4.36
.

For
G
not semisimple, let us drop the assumption
\beta\equiv 0
. If
{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)_{|{V\setminus\{0\}}}}\neq 0
, then it is necessarily of the form
i^{\prime}_{+}\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}
for some
\ell\in\mathds{Z}
as before, since
\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}_{L^{*}}
-module of rank one.
One can also show that
\mathscr{N}_{L^{*}}^{\beta}\cong\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}
forces
k\beta=\differential\chi
for some group character
\chi\colon G^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}^{*}
.
With the same arguments as in the proofs above, we then get:

{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)_{|{V\setminus\{0\}}}}\cong\begin{cases}i^{\prime}_{+}\mathcal{O}_{L^{*}}^{\ell/k}&\quad\text{if $\exists\ell\in\mathds{Z}:\beta(\mathbf{e})=\ell/k$ and $\mathscr{L}^{\otimes\ell}\{\chi\}\cong\omega_{X}^{\otimes(-k)}$ }\\
&\quad\text{as $G$-equivariant line bundles,}\\
\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Hence, in the general situation we still only get a non-zero
{\hat{\tau}(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)_{|{V\setminus\{0\}}}}
for
\mathscr{L}
being a rational power of the anticanonical bundle and for exactly one suitable
\beta
uniquely determined by
\mathscr{L}
.

5
Representation theoretic criterion

The purpose of this section is to give a necessary criterion for the non-vanishing of tautological systems in terms of the representation of
G
on the space
V=H^{0}(X,\mathcal{L})^{\vee}
, at least in the case where
G
is semi-simple. On the one hand, this is a slight sharpening of one direction of
4.34
, which only concerns vanishing of
\hat{\tau}(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)
on
V\backslash\{0\}
. On the other hand, the methods used
here heavily rely on the representation theory of semi-simple Lie algebras, and this section is therefore in large parts logically independent of the rest of the paper.

5.1
Formula for
\beta(\mathbf{e})

We work in the same setup as in
4.34
. Additionally, we assume throughout this section

G
is semisimple.

This implies automatically that any Lie algebra homomorphism
\beta\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\to\mathds{C}
satisfies
{\beta_{|{\mathfrak{g}}}}=0
, since
\mathfrak{g}=[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]
. In particular, the choice of
\beta
is equivalent to the choice of a complex number
\beta(\mathbf{e})
. As we will see, the tautological system
\tau(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)
will only be non-zero for particular values of
\beta(\mathbf{e})
that we can express in terms of the highest weight of the (necessarily irreducible by Borel–Weil, see e.g.
[
Ser54
]
or
[
Bot57
, Cor. to Th. V]
or also
5.11
below)
G
-representation
\rho
.

To state this formula, let
\mathfrak{t}
be a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
, let
\Phi^{+}\subseteq\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
be a choice of positive roots, and set

\delta\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\lambda\in\Phi^{+}}\lambda.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\mu
be the highest weight of the irreducible
G
-representation
V\coloneqq H^{0}(X,\mathscr{L})^{\vee}
. If
\tau(\rho,\hat{X},\beta)
is nonzero, then

\beta(\mathbf{e})\in\Big\{0,\,\frac{2\langle\delta,\mu\rangle}{|\mu|^{2}}\Big\},

where
\langle-,-\rangle
is the inner product on
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
dual to (the restriction to
\mathfrak{t}
